Each copies every row the answer holds, in the current sort order, and then reads its result until the next run: `Copied {rows} rows as JSON.` or `Copied {rows} rows as a table.` On failure: `This browser did not let the page write to the clipboard.` How a cell is written is section 2.10 rule 5. Row 5 adds this part |

**No cell is tinted by value.** A colour that means "bad" needs a rule, and this page cannot have one: it does not know which of the registry's ledgers' values are bad. The bar in rule 6 is a comparison a reader makes; a red cell is a verdict nobody issued.

**This page prints what the engine returned.** Column names as the ledger spells them, decimals, `null`, and dates as `YYYY-MM-DD`, in a plain table; a chart's axis title is the column's name. The operator wrote the question and types these names and values back into the next one, so translating them would break it. Six console rules give way here, and row 4 writes the exemption into `docs/concepts/console-design.md` and `docs/concepts/console-design/the-rules-every-console-chart-obeys.md`: no ledger column name on screen, no decimals, a dash for null, the `Quantity, unit` header form, the `27 Sep 2026` date style, and "a generic Table component is refused". The page's charts keep every other rule (Susan and Jony, 2026-10-02). **How a cell prints is Susan's rule 4**: Jony's version printed every value as the engine's text with no thousands separators, and the words are Susan's call; her five-digit floor keeps a year ungrouped.

**The chart panel**, `data-explorer-shape`, titled `The answer, drawn` (row 5). The chart type is chosen from the answer's own columns, first match wins. The floors of the spread and paired charts are the ones their components already carry, because the chart-shapes page calls fewer rows too few to mean anything (Susan, 2026-10-02).

| # | The answer holds | Type, and its option word and icon | Floor | Bound |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | one date or timestamp column, at least one numeric, and one row per UTC day | `dateSeries`, `Over time`, `shape-series` | `console.explorer_chart_min_rows` UTC days | at most four number columns drawn, in `--series-1` to `--series-4` |
| 2 | one text column and exactly one numeric, with no negative value | `rankedList`, `Ranked`, `shape-ranked` | - | `console.explorer_rank_max` rows drawn |
| 3 | exactly two numeric columns, and at most one text column | `pairedScatter`, `Paired`, `shape-scatter` | `console.fleet_min_rows` rows and `console.bandwidth_min_kinds` subjects | - |
| 4 | exactly one numeric column | `distribution`, `Spread`, `shape-distribution` | `console.fleet_min_rows` rows | - |
| 5 | none of the above | no tile checked; the box says `The page does not pick a chart for these columns. Choose one under Draw it as.`, or, where no chart can be filled, `Nothing here to draw: the answer has no number in it.` (row 19) | - | - |

| # | Rule about the chart panel |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | **The date chart needs one row per UTC day in its `Date` column.** With several, it draws nothing and the box says `Nothing here to draw: the answer has several rows a UTC day in "{date}". Group by day in the question to draw it over time.` `Lines` holds one to four number columns, one for each of the explorer's series colours, drawn in answer order with the first in the first colour; by default the number columns whose largest value is at least `console.explorer_series_floor_share` (0.05) of the largest column's, the first four in answer order. While the lines are the page's own, the foot names the ones left out as flat: `"{name}" is left out: it is under {share}% of "{largest}", so it would draw flat.` for one, and `{n} number columns are left out: each is under {share}% of "{largest}", so each would draw flat.` for several; once the reader changes a line, that sentence goes. The `Lines` pill shows what is drawn, so no sentence names the first four (row 19, Susan's D2 and D3). The closest pair of `--series-1` to `--series-4` is 17.5 CIEDE2000 colour-difference units apart in both themes |
| 2 | **The ranked chart** ends with `{n} more rows are in the table.` when the answer holds more rows than it draws |
| 3 | **The paired chart's subject** is the one text column; with no text column, each row is its own subject. The spread and paired charts keep their own "too few" sentences |
| 4 | **Below a floor** the panel draws `too-few` with `tooFewSentence()` from `waiting.ts`, as every console chart does |
| 5 | **Every type the explorer draws is a tile on every answer, in one order, and every tile can be pressed**: `Over time`, `Ranked`, `Paired`, `Spread`, and from row 20 `Side by side`, `Which days` and `Flow`. No tile is hidden, greyed or disabled: a hidden tile moves its neighbours on the next run, and the arrow keys skip a disabled radio, which gives no reason. A type the answer cannot draw draws nothing, and the chart box says what it needs in one sentence (chart rule 10). Before any answer and any press, no tile is checked. A checked tile is exactly as wide as an unchecked one. The legend is `Draw it as`, radio tiles, each an icon then a word (the table above), at the strip's end while the Chart tab is open (row 10's R2), and the tiles are `ChoiceTiles.svelte`. Each role of the chosen type is a pill above the drawing that opens a filtered list of the answer's columns its family fits (row 19). Each chart's accessible name is `{option word}: {columns}` (Susan, 2026-10-07, replacing "only types that fit are shown, and when only one fits no switch is drawn") |
| 6 | **No sentence names the type or argues for it**: the tiles and the pills show the choice, and R4 keeps text off the space above the chart (Susan's D1, row 19). The foot under the drawing says `Drawn from the first {max} rows.` when the answer is capped, because the table's status line is out of sight on the Chart tab, and names the rows a date chart leaves out because their day is NULL |
| 7 | **Main figure and comparison**, for sufficiency gates 3 and 5. The main figure carries `data-lede` in `--text-xl`, stands above the chart, and is the panel's one `data-lede`: the panel passes no `lede` to `DateSeries`. Date: `{value} {column} on {shortDate(newest)}`, compared as `{column} on each day against the other days in the span`. Ranked: `{label}: {value} {column}`, compared as `each {label column} against the largest`. Spread: `Half of {column} is at or under {median}`, compared as `each band of {column} against the share of rows at or below it`. Paired: `{rows} rows of {y} against {x}`, compared as `{y} against {x}`. The date chart also carries `data-model-rule="no"` and `data-model-rule-none="this page does not know which settings changed inside your span"` (gate 6) |
| 8 | **Readout strips**, record by record. The date chart starts on the newest day, as it already does. The spread chart starts on its tallest bin; Left and Right step through the bins; the text reads `{x0} to {x1}: {count} rows, {share}% at or below`. The paired chart starts on the point with the largest y; Left and Right step in x order; the text reads `{subject}: {x} {xv}, {y} {yv}`. The ranked chart carries `data-readout-none="every row prints its own value beside its bar; agreed with Susan"`. Names are the column names, and values use the table's cell format (answer-panel rule 4) |
| 9 | **What the shared chart components gain.** `Distribution.svelte` and `PairedScatter.svelte` lose the SVG `<title>` on every mark, which gate 4 and the readout rule both refuse, and gain the record-by-record readout strip. `RankedList.svelte` gains `data-chart-type="rankedList"` on its root element, so gate 1 sees a plot |
| 10 | **Where the chosen or opening chart cannot draw the answer, the panel draws no chart and one neutral sentence in its box, carrying `data-shape-none`, and keeps its height.** With no chart picked, the box says `The page does not pick a chart for these columns. Choose one under Draw it as.`, which replaces the old advice to keep one or two columns in the question. Where no chart can be filled - in row 19, no number column - it says `Nothing here to draw: the answer has no number in it.` whichever tile is checked. A chart whose roles these columns cannot fill says its own sentence, the same whichever role is empty: `Nothing here to draw: Over time needs a date or timestamp column for Date, and a number column for Lines.`, `Nothing here to draw: Ranked needs a number column to rank by, and one more column for Name.`, `Nothing here to draw: Paired needs two number columns, one for Across and one for Up.` or `Nothing here to draw: Spread needs a number column for Values.`, and a date chart with no date column adds `The ledgers keep their dates as text: CAST(date AS DATE) in the question makes a date column.` Row 19's Table C holds the sentences for a pick that cannot draw. It stays; it is not a dismissible alarm, because it is a normal outcome. It is not `refused`, which means a question that did not run (section 2.12) |

### 2.12 The states, and which vocabulary owns each

`frontend/src/lib/console/waiting.ts` owns `PanelState = 'ready' | 'loading' | 'quiet' | 'missing' | 'unreachable'` and `ChartState = PanelState | 'too-few'`. **Row 2 adds one member and nothing else** (Guardrail #4): `refused` joins `PanelState` and the table of states in `waiting.ts`, and `frontend/src/lib/charts/d3/empty.ts`, which re-exports each state's sentence, gains `refusedSentence`. Row 6 names `refused` in sufficiency gate 8 in `docs/concepts/design-system.md`, and each judged explorer panel's driver drives it.

| # | State | Where it lives | What it means here |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `loading` | `PanelState`, exists | The engine, the indexes or the files are on their way |
| 2 | `quiet` | `PanelState`, exists | The statement ran and matched no rows |
| 3 | `missing` | `PanelState`, exists | A chosen ledger is declared but not in `__PUBLISHED_LEDGERS__`, so the page asks the site nothing for it; or `ask()` found no `daily.json` for it, which is the door's `not-packed` |
| 4 | `unreachable` | `PanelState`, exists | A date in the span that no tier can answer, a file that could not be had, or an engine that could not start - the causes `slice()` already answers `unreachable` for. `AskFault` (section 2.4) says which |
| 5 | `refused` | **`PanelState` gains it, row 2** | The statement did not run, or the engine rejected it. `statement.ts` refused it, the fetch ceiling refused it before any data file was fetched, or the engine answered the statement with an error, whose message is the reason. It is shown as text. It is not a fetch that failed, which is what `unreachable` already means. It draws in the neutral tone, because `empty.ts` gives the warn tone to `unreachable` alone. A chart panel with nothing to draw is not `refused` (section 2.11, chart-panel rule 10), and row 2 says so on the chart-shapes page |
| 6 | `capped` | **not a state.** A boolean on `AskResult.state === 'ok'` | The answer reached `maxRows` and stopped. The rows shown are real; the answer is not the whole one. The panel is `ready` and its note says so (section 2.11, answer-panel rule 5) |
| 7 | Nothing has run yet | **not a state.** The page's no-answer state, carrying `data-explorer-idle` | Before the first run in this page's life. It is not `quiet`, which means a run matched nothing, and its words differ from `quiet`'s (Jony and Susan, 2026-10-02) |

**`capped` is a flag and not a sixth state on purpose.** A capped answer has rows to draw and a state is what a panel draws *instead of* rows. Spelling it as a state would have put one condition in two vocabularies.

**The words each state prints on this page** (Susan, 2026-10-02). The sentences `waiting.ts` already holds talk about months and windows - "Nothing was recorded in these 14 days." - which is false when a filter in the question matched nothing, so row 4 adds these to `waiting.ts` for this page. The warn tint goes to a failed fetch and a failed engine only.

| # | When | The answer panel | The chart panel (row 5) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Nothing has run yet | A neutral box `console.chart_height` tall: `Press Run and the answer appears here, as a table.` | The same box: `If the answer holds a number, it is drawn here.` |
| 2 | `loading`, and while a run is running | The shimmer box, no words | The shimmer box, no words |
| 3 | `quiet` | `Your question ran and matched no rows.` | `No rows, so nothing to draw.` |
| 4 | `missing`: not published | `{ledger} is not on this site yet, so nothing was asked of it.` | `Nothing was asked, so nothing to draw.` |
| 5 | `missing`: no days yet (`not-packed`) | `{ledger} has no days on this site yet.` | `Nothing was asked, so nothing to draw.` |
| 6 | `unreachable`: a file did not arrive (`fault` null) | Warn tint: `{ledger} for {shortDate(day)} did not arrive, so the question did not run.`, and the button `Try {shortDate(day)} again` | Warn tint: `The answer did not arrive, so nothing to draw.` |
| 7 | `unreachable`: a gap in the files (`index-missing`, `file-missing`, `day-missing`) | Warn tint, no button: `No file on this site holds {ledger} for {shortDate(day)}.` | As row 6 |
| 8 | `unreachable`: the engine did not start (`fault` is `engine`), including in a browser that cannot run it | Warn tint: `The query engine did not start in this browser. A current version of Chrome, Edge, Firefox or Safari runs it.`, and the button `Start the engine again` | Warn tint: `The question did not run, so nothing to draw.` |
| 9 | `refused` | Neutral, drawn in place of the table, and it cannot be dismissed. Two statements: `Run one statement at a time. This has {n}.` Not read-only: `Only questions run here: start with SELECT, WITH, DESCRIBE, SUMMARIZE or EXPLAIN. This starts with {WORD}.` Too long: `This question is {n} characters long; the most this page runs is {max}.` Over the fetch ceiling: `These days would fetch {mb} from {f} files, and one question may fetch {max}. Pick fewer days or fewer ledgers.` The engine's error: `The engine could not run this:`, then the message word for word in `--font-data`, as plain text, keeping its line breaks and spaces in a box that scrolls sideways (`white-space: pre; overflow-x: auto`), so the caret stays under the word it points at, at every width | Neutral: `The question did not run, so nothing to draw.` |

### 2.13 The knobs this plan mints or moves

Every value below is a knob (Guardrail #6), in `config/appearance.json` with its model in `backend/idhazh/contracts/knobs/console.py` unless stated. **The `Row` column is the one that mints or moves it; no knob is read by a row before the row that mints it.** A knob a model gains is also named in its committed config file (`test_the_config_file_names_every_knob_it_owns`) and in every contract fixture of that model, because a fixture dumps every field and must round-trip byte for byte (`test_fixture_round_trips_byte_identically`). `ConsoleConfig` sits in both config models - `AppearanceConfig.console` and the legacy `AppConfig.console` - so a console knob joins both `tests/fixtures/contracts/appearance-config/knobs-set-away-from-the-defaults.json` and `tests/fixtures/contracts/app-config/every-knob-differs-from-the-committed-config.json`; a ledger knob joins the second, and an icons knob the first.

| Key | Row | Status | Value | Read by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `console.explorer_chrome` | 8 | **New** | `"workbench"` | The Data explorer route asks the console layout for compact workbench chrome. Setting it to `"console"` restores the full console chrome without a source edit. |
| `console.default_window_days` | 1 | **Exists at 30, becomes 14**, in the model default, in `config/appearance.json`, in the frontend fallback in `frontend/src/lib/server/config.ts`, and in the legacy `console` block of `config/idhazh.json` | `14` | Every console route opens on it; `bundle-gate.mjs` and `test_page_ceilings.py` read the legacy copy in `config/idhazh.json` to price a cold console load, and `test_a_moved_block_is_declared_in_one_file_and_the_other_does_not_argue` holds the two copies equal. It is already in `window_presets`, so the model's own validator passes unchanged |
| `page_weight.cold_console_load_bytes` | 1 | **Exists at 3,400,000, becomes 2,300,000**, in `config/idhazh.json` | `2300000` | `test_the_committed_config_caps_what_a_cold_console_load_fetches` requires it to be at least the worst case and below the worst case plus one telemetry shard. At 14 days a window touches at most 2 month shards, so the worst case is 2,000 (`console/band.json`) + 2 x 1,100,000 (`telemetry/`) = 2,202,000, and the ceiling must stay under 3,302,000. 2,300,000 keeps the 98,000 bytes of room the value has over its worst case at 30 days today |

The route hands it to nobody: its one group is untitled, and an untitled group draws no jump link |

| `console.explorer_readout_lines` | 9 | **New; phone value moved by R7** | `[4, 3, 4, 3]` | The lines the status bar reserves, per frame band. Text past that scrolls inside the fixed bar. R7 shortened the answered and quiet sentences. The longest real sentence after R7 is `Run reads 123 files, 64.0 MB from 4 ledgers over 90 UTC days. It also starts the query engine.`, and M11 measures it at 390 px: it fits in 4 reserved lines. |

Jony and Susan, 2026-10-04 |

| `console.explorer_max_fetch_bytes` | 4 | **New** | `67108864` (64 MiB), **an estimate** | Row 4 passes it as `AskOptions.maxFetchBytes`. The estimate: every packed file of every packed ledger summed to 9,796,245 bytes on 2026-10-02, from their committed indexes, so this refuses no span possible today, and a wide archive span that reaches a year file is the first thing it bounds. **Row 4 takes one reading**: on a local build of the site from the committed `state/` (not the canary, whose ledgers are a few kilobytes), in Chromium, with the CPU slowed four times through CDP `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate`, it runs `SELECT count(*)` over every published ledger at the widest preset and records the bytes fetched, the time from press to answer, and any main-thread long task over 1,000 ms. If that run stalls, the knob drops below the stalling total; if it does not, the estimate stands and the reading is recorded beside it as the largest span measured. Owned by Guardrail #10: a ceiling is not lowered below a span that was seen to work |
| `console.explorer_query_max_chars` | 4 | **New** | `4000`, **an estimate**; row 5 replaces it from a reading | Row 4 passes it as `AskOptions.maxChars`; row 5 sizes the link from it. The estimate: 4,000 characters that do not compress at all deflate to about 4,000 bytes and base64url-encode to about 5,340 characters, which with the `ledgers` and `days` parameters fits an 8,192-byte request line, a common host default. **Row 5's reading**: it requests `/console/data-explorer/?q=<n characters>` on the live Pages site for growing `n`, by doubling and then halving the step, and takes the longest address answered 200 or 404 rather than 414, 431 or 400; it then sets the knob to the longest statement whose worst-case encoding fits that address with every ledger name in `ledgers`, and records the method and the date in `docs/reference/benchmarks/address-length-on-pages.md`. **The arithmetic assumes one byte per character**, and a character outside ASCII takes up to three: a statement written in ASCII that fits the box always fits a link, and one with other characters can still meet row 5's address rule 5 |
| `console.explorer_chart_min_rows` | 5 | **New** | `3` | The fewest UTC days a date chart draws (section 2.11, the chart panel's type table): two points define a line, so a chart of two is a claim. The spread and paired charts keep the floors their components already carry, `console.fleet_min_rows` and `console.bandwidth_min_kinds` |
| `console.explorer_rank_max` | 5 | **New** | `30` | The most rows a `rankedList` draws |
| `console.explorer_saved_max` | 5 | **New** | `20` | How many saved questions this browser keeps. The oldest goes when it is full and the page says which |
| `console.explorer_history_max` | 5 | **New** | `10` | How many recent runs the list holds |
| `console.explorer_save_name_max_chars` | 5 | **New** | `40` | The longest name Save fills in from the statement's first line (section 2.16 rule 10). Fowler, 2026-10-02 |
| `console.explorer_series_floor_share` | 5 | **New** | `0.05` | A date-chart series whose largest value is under this share of the largest series' is left out and named in the note (section 2.11, chart-panel rule 1). Fowler, 2026-10-02 |
| `console.explorer_examples` | 4 | **New** | the five in section 2.14 | The question strip. A list of `{id, title, ledgers, days, sql}`: the `note` an earlier draft carried is dropped, because the title already says what the question asks (Jony, 2026-10-02) |
| `console.judged_panel_ids` | 6 | Exists as `["platform-mix"]` | gains `data-explorer-rows` and `data-explorer-shape` | Row 6. The question panel is not judged: it is a form, so it fails gates 1, 3 and 5 whatever is built, and the gate test cannot exempt one gate (Susan, 2026-10-02; row 6 decision 6) |
| `icons.stroke_px` | 6 | **New**, in the `icons` block, model `IconsConfig` in `backend/idhazh/contracts/appearance_config.py` | `1.25` until the comparison sheet says otherwise | **The line width in screen pixels, the same at every icon size.** `Icon.svelte` draws `stroke-width = stroke_px * 24 / size`, because Lucide's stroke is in units of a 24-unit grid: a fixed 1.5 units would be 0.81 px at 13 px, 0.88 at 14, 1.00 at 16 and 1.25 at 20. It reaches `Icon.svelte`, which prerendered pages also use, as the build-time constant `__ICON_STROKE_PX__` in `frontend/vite.config.ts` and `frontend/src/app.d.ts`; nothing calls `iconsConfig()` today. Today's icons range from 1.08 to 1.67 px. Susan picks the value from one comparison sheet (row 6 decision 4) |
| `ledger.published` | 3 | Exists: `host-fingerprint`, `item-health`, `summary-quality-evals` (#1169) | every ledger whose three compact indexes are committed at the row's base commit: `git ls-files 'state/compact/*/index/*.json'`. On 2026-10-02 that names seven ledgers: `candidate-models`, `counterfactual-scores`, `host-fingerprint`, `item-health`, `published`, `seen` and `summary-quality-evals` | `published-ledgers.mjs` through `copy-visuals.mjs`, the canary build, `test_page_ceilings.py`, the gardener's loader, and `__PUBLISHED_LEDGERS__` |
| `ledger.archive_base_url` | 3 | **New**, in `config/idhazh.json` beside `ledger.published` | ships **empty**, which means the site only | Row 6's archive keeper and `asset-base.js`'s `connect-src`. **Declared by row 3 and first read by row 6**. Its validator is the shape `engine_extension_repository` already carries, with the empty string also allowed |
| `page_weight.payload_ceilings_bytes` | 3 | Gains entries, in `config/idhazh.json` | sized by the existing tests | One `state/compact/<ledger>/index/` key per newly published ledger, at least twice the heavier of its longest day and month index (`test_every_published_ledger_bounds_its_indexes_at_twice_their_longest` computes it; each of the three published today holds 2,200), and one `config/ledgers.json` key at least twice the registry's gzipped size. The writers' listings take no key: that test holds the `state/` keys to exactly one index key per published ledger |

**How the page reads them.** Every `console.explorer_*` knob reaches the page in `__EXPLORER_CONFIG__`, through `explorerConfig()`, and stays out of `ConsoleConfig` (section 2.3 fact 6), so each one joins `explorerConfig()` and its fallback in `frontend/src/lib/server/config.ts` in the row that mints it, beside `config/appearance.json`, `knobs/console.py` and both contract fixtures.

**Read here and unchanged:** `console.chart_height` (the height of the empty boxes before a run), `console.fleet_min_rows` and `console.bandwidth_min_kinds` (the spread and paired charts' floors), `console.shimmer_after_ms`, `frame.breakpoints_px` (640 px and 1024 px are the two widths this page switches at), `icons.size_px`, and `ui.repo_url` (the how-to link, row 6).

**The six code colours are theme tokens, not knobs in `config/appearance.json`**, as every colour of the palette is: `--code-ground`, `--code-text`, `--code-keyword`, `--code-string`, `--code-number` and `--code-comment`, written out for each theme in `frontend/src/styles/tokens.css` beside `--chart-*` (section 2.16 rule 4), and `frontend/tests/tokens.spec.ts` holds each at 4.5:1 or more against its ground (row 4).

### 2.14 The example questions

Five, in `console.explorer_examples`, each `{id, title, ledgers, days, sql}`. **Row 4 writes each `sql` against its ledgers' contracts**, and row 4's spec runs every example whose ledgers the canary build publishes. Pressing a chip puts its statement, ledgers and span into the editor as one edit that can be undone, sets the span as pressing a span tile does, and runs nothing (section 2.16 rule 3). **A chip whose ledgers are not all published is not drawn** (Jony, 2026-10-02): showing it was meant to preview later questions. On 2026-10-02 `feed-health` holds no packed indexes, so the strip opens with four examples, and `feeds-gone-quiet` appears with the config edit that publishes `feed-health` (row 3 decision 11). Example 5 answers one row per UTC day, so the strip always holds one question the chart panel draws over time (Susan, 2026-10-02).

| # | id | Title | Ledgers | Days |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `p99-by-machine` | p99 job time by machine kind | `host-fingerprint` | 14 |
| 2 | `throughput-by-machine` | Prompt throughput by machine kind | `host-fingerprint` | 14 |
| 3 | `feeds-gone-quiet` | Feeds with no good fetch in the span | `feed-health` | 14 |
| 4 | `why-items-failed` | What failed to summarize, and why | `item-health` | 14 |
| 5 | `scored-per-day` | Summaries scored, day by day | `summary-quality-evals` | 14 |

**The observability words stay the observability words** (owner, 2026-09-28): `p50`, `p99`, throughput, cardinality, dimension.

### 2.15 What the page takes from the reference, and the three rules that decided it

The owner's reference is a query workbench screenshot and its HTML: `code.html`, `DESIGN.md` and `screen.png` under `test-results/telemetry_parquet_query_visualizer/`. **`test-results/` is ignored by git** (`.gitignore` line 23), so the reference is not committed and exists only in the owner's main checkout; a worker in a fresh worktree does not have it. Row 6's worker reads it there, read-only, or works from this section alone, which carries every verdict the inventory needs. The feature-by-feature inventory with a verdict and a reason per feature is [docs/concepts/console-design/what-the-data-explorer-borrowed.md](../docs/concepts/console-design/what-the-data-explorer-borrowed.md), written by row 6. Three rules decided every rejection and they are here because they also decide anything added later.

| # | Rule | What it rejected |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **A number on this page is a reading or it is not printed.** | `Scanned 6.8 GB`, `AVX-512`, `PARALLEL THREADS: 12`, `Arrow IPC Cache Hit: 94.1%`. Rows 4 and 5 print milliseconds, files, bytes and rows, each measured |
| 2 | **The console owns a job once.** | The left icon rail, the breadcrumb, the collapse-all. The tab strip, the span control and the panel frame already exist |
| 3 | **A verdict needs a rule.** | The green and red status badges, the value coloured red for being high, the animated ring on the point labelled an anomaly. None has a rule behind it that this page could hold |

**The glyphs are taken; the font is not.** The reference draws Material Symbols from a Google font. This repository already has an icon system - Lucide, ISC licence, committed as source SVG, drawn from a generated sprite by id, with `frontend/tests/icons.spec.ts` failing both on a glyph nothing draws and on an id that does not exist. **So each mark joins that set in the row that first draws it**, and row 6 sets one line weight for the whole set (`icons.stroke_px`, section 2.13). Names describe meaning, not shape, as the icon PROVENANCE file requires. All fourteen were checked to exist in lucide-static 0.544.0 (Susan, 2026-10-02).

| # | Id | From Lucide | Drawn on | Row |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `query-run` | `play` | Run | 4 |
| 2 | `list-refresh` | `refresh-cw` | Refresh | 4 |
| 3 | `sort-ascending` | `arrow-up` | a sorted column's header | 4 |
| 4 | `sort-descending` | `arrow-down` | a sorted column's header | 4 |
| 5 | `copy` | `copy` | the three copy buttons: `Copy as JSON`, `Copy as table` and `Copy question` | 5 |
| 6 | `saved` | `bookmark` | Save, and the first mark on a saved chip, so your own questions look different from the examples | 5 |
| 7 | `forget` | `x` | the labelled remove button on a saved chip | 5 |
| 8 | `history` | `history` | the recent-runs disclosure | 5 |
| 9 | `share-link` | `link` | `Copy link` | 5 |
| 10 | `shape-series` | `chart-line` | the `Over time` option | 5 |
| 11 | `shape-ranked` | `chart-bar` | the `Ranked` option | 5 |
| 12 | `shape-distribution` | `chart-column` | the `Spread` option | 5 |
| 13 | `shape-scatter` | `chart-scatter` | the `Paired` option | 5 |
| 14 | `docs` | `book-open` | the how-to link in the question panel's note | 6 |

`search` (the ledger filter) and `external` (after the how-to link) are reused. The fourteen files add about 5.5 KB to today's 6,567 bytes, well under the 40 KB budget, with one provenance file and no third-party request. **Each is written as a literal `<Icon id="...">`**, with `{#if}` choosing between the two sort marks and among the four shape marks, because `icons.spec.ts` finds a computed id only when it starts with `band-`, `topic-` or `theme-`.

### 2.16 The question panel, part by part

**2026-10-04 redesign amendment, updated by owner on 2026-10-06.** Rows 8 to 10 rebuild this panel as a workbench. Row 8 moves the span control, the dates and Run into a toolbar at the top of the panel, in place of the route-level span sentence. Row 9 replaces the growing editor with a fixed one, the action line with a fixed status bar, the flow history with a floating disclosure, and the 70svh table box with fixed answer and chart regions, each with the knob that sizes it. The column rail is B1: it always shows the selected ledgers' columns, and Run does not change it.

**2026-10-05 amendment (row 12).** The owner asked for a page that fills the window with no fixed size, Run beside Save and Copy link, and the copy buttons placed where they overlap nothing. Row 12 amends rules 2, 3, 5, 6 and 10 and row 9's fixed regions. The workbench fills the window below the strip, and its regions share it through `fr` tracks with usable minimums instead of fixed sizes. From 1024 px the side columns are `minmax(12rem, 1fr)` beside a `minmax(0, 4fr)` editor, not `console.explorer_rail_rem` wide (rule 2). Run stands in one group with Save and Copy link at the right end of the editor's heading line, not under the editor and not in the toolbar (rules 5 and 6). `Copy link` stands in that group, not on the panel's heading line, and `Copy as JSON` and `Copy as table` stand on the answer's own visible heading line, one control tall (rule 10). From 640 px the question strip is one line, and the chips that do not fit fold into `{n} more`, so saving a question never makes it taller (rule 3). Row 12's decisions say how.

`data-explorer-ask`, titled `Your question`. The places are Jony's and the words Susan's, both 2026-10-02; where their reviews disagreed, the rule names who decided. Row 4 builds every rule below unless the rule names a later row.

| # | Part | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | The panel's note | `One read-only DuckDB statement at a time, up to {explorer_query_max_chars} characters.` From row 6 a link follows it on the same line, drawn through `Panel`'s `noteEnd` snippet, not a button: the `docs` icon, `How to ask a question`, then the `external` icon, to `{ui.repo_url}/blob/main/docs/how-to/query-a-ledger-from-the-console.md`, with `rel="noopener"`, because the how-to page lives in `docs/`, which the site does not publish. No note repeats the cross-link sentence or the tab's description. Jony put the link in the note, because going to another page is a link; Susan wrote its words and marks |
| 2 | Two arrangements | **The panel switches at `frame.breakpoints_px[1]` (1024 px)**, the width where the strip starts to stick. Below it, one column, in this order: the question strip; the ledgers, as a `<details>` whose summary names the chosen ones (`Ledgers: host-fingerprint, item-health`) and which is open only while none is chosen; the editor; the action line; recent runs (row 5); the columns, as a closed `<details>` summarised `Columns ({n})`. From 1024 px: the question strip across the full panel width, then three columns - on the left, the ledger list in a side column `console.explorer_rail_rem` wide; in the middle, the editor, the action line and recent runs; on the right, the column list in a second side column of the same width. Both side columns are open and each scrolls inside its own box, so neither makes the panel taller than the editor column; neither has a border or a surface of its own. The three columns stand `--space-5` (24 px) apart. The editor is then about 414 px wide at 1024 px - about 40 characters of `--font-data` at 16 px once the line numbers take their place - and about 830 px, about 90 characters, at 1440 px; these are estimates from Consolas at 8.8 px a character (a reading, 2026-10-02), and a wider font fits fewer. Keyboard order is the visual order. One component draws each list at both widths. Below 1024 px it sits in a `<details>` and the summary takes the place of its heading, so `Ledgers` and `Columns` are not printed twice; `Refresh` stands at the start of the open list. From 1024 px, matched with `matchMedia` on `frame.breakpoints_px[1]`, it is drawn under its own heading with no `<details>` and no summary. Crossing that width keeps what is chosen and what is typed in the filter. Susan proposed 640 px for the switch; the breakpoint is Jony's call |
| 3 | The question strip | **One strip of questions, the first line of the panel at every width, across its full width** - one component, `QuestionStrip.svelte`. It holds two labelled runs: `Saved`, this browser's questions, newest first (row 5), then `Examples`. A run with no chips has no label, so before anything is saved the strip shows `Examples` alone. It wraps and never scrolls sideways; after `console.explorer_strip_shown[0]` chips below 640 px, or `[1]` from 640 px, the rest sit in a `<details>` at its end, labelled `{n} more`. A chip is a button that shows its title, at least 2.75rem tall, with the title in `--text-sm`; a title wider than its row wraps inside the chip. Pressing it puts its statement, ledgers and span into the editor as one edit that can be undone (Ctrl+Z gives back what was typed), sets the span as pressing a span tile does, and never runs. A saved chip starts with the `saved` mark and ends with an icon button, `forget`, labelled `Forget {name}`. When the saved list is full, a line under the strip reads `Saved "{new}". "{oldest}" was the oldest of {max} and is no longer kept.` until the next save. A chip whose ledgers are not all published is not drawn (section 2.14) |
| 4 | The editor | Visible label `Your question, in DuckDB SQL`. A `textarea` with a highlighted layer behind it (row 4 decision 5), filling the width of its column, in `--font-data` at `--workbench-field-text` and `--workbench-field-leading`. It is exactly `console.explorer_editor_lines_shown[0]` lines below 1024 px and `[1]` lines from 1024 px, then scrolls inside. **Long lines wrap** (Jony; Susan had proposed `wrap="off"`), and the highlighted layer is drawn one row per statement line, with that line's number in the same row, so a number sits on the first row of its line and cannot drift from it. The numbers earn their place because the engine's errors name a line (`LINE 3:`). They are in the tertiary colour, tabular and `aria-hidden`; the colour layer is `aria-hidden` and scrolls with the box. Background `--code-ground`, a 1 px `--color-rule-strong` border, `--radius-md`. Spellcheck, autocorrect, autocapitalize and autocomplete are off. Focus: a 2 px `--color-focus` outline, offset 2 px, on the frame, through `:focus-within`. Tab and Shift+Tab move focus out of the box; it does not trap Tab. Placeholder: `Write one DuckDB question, or pick an example above.` The fixed status bar carries the hint and counter when needed. **The code colours**, each at least 4.5:1 against its ground: dark, on `#0f131b` - text `#e6edf5` 15.8:1, keyword `#a5a5ff` 8.4:1, string `#4fc7dd` 9.3:1, number `#f08cc4` 8.2:1, comment `#78859a` 5.0:1; light, on `#ffffff`, where the border marks the box - text `#101828` 17.8:1, keyword `#3f37c9` 8.1:1, string `#0e7c8e` 4.9:1, number `#b03a86` 5.5:1, comment `#667085` 5.0:1. The palette's own colours do not serve here: on the light sunken ground the tertiary text measures 4.40:1 and `--chart-3` 4.33:1 |
| 5 | The action line | **Directly under the editor at every width.** At its start, one sentence about the next run, whose words follow the moment: before a run, `Run fetches {f} files, {mb} for {k} ledgers over {d} days.`, with `It also starts the query engine.` before the first run; while a chosen ledger's columns are fetched, `Choosing a ledger fetches one day of it to list its columns.`; during a run, `Starting the query engine.`, then `Fetching {f} files, {mb}.`, then `Running the question.`; after it, `Answered in {t}. Fetched {f} files, {mb}; {h} more files were already in this page.`, with `See the answer`, a link to the answer panel drawn only while the top of that panel is off screen - the page never scrolls by itself. Once the page holds a fetched file, the line goes on: `This page holds {mb} of fetched files; a reload empties it.` These replace one another in one place and never stack. At every width the action line is two rows. The first is the sentence, in `--text-sm`, across the full width of its column, wrapping as it needs. The second has `From (UTC)` and `To (UTC)` at its start, with labels above them (row 6), and at its right end `Save` (row 5) and then `Run`, aligned to the bottom of the row. A sentence measures up to 871 px at `--text-sm` (a reading, 2026-10-02), so it cannot share a row with the buttons in a 414 px or an 830 px column (Jony, 2026-10-02). The sentence is `aria-live="polite"`, and focus stays where it was. MB means 1,048,576 bytes, to one decimal place, as `megabytes()` in `frontend/src/lib/assist/session.ts` prints it; KB below 0.1 MB; milliseconds below 1 s, otherwise seconds to one decimal. While Run is disabled the sentence gives the reason instead: `Pick at least one ledger to ask.` or `Write a question to run.` A link's notices stand here too, before the price, until the first run (row 5's address rules). The date inputs sit in the second row because they change what the sentence prices and what Run reads (section 2.8) |
| 6 | Run | Filled `--color-accent` with `--color-on-accent` text (6.5:1 dark, 6.3:1 light), the `query-run` icon and the word `Run`, 2.75rem tall, with `aria-keyshortcuts="Control+Enter Meta+Enter"`. Ctrl+Enter, or Cmd+Enter on a Mac, in the editor presses it. It is disabled until a published ledger is chosen and the editor holds text. While a run is in progress it reads `Running` and is disabled, and the answer and chart panels show the shimmer box with no words. Run stands under the editor, not at the top right as in the reference, because the price belongs beside the button it prices |
| 7 | The column list | `ColumnList.svelte` - not `SchemaPanel`, because on this console a "panel" is the framed section. Heading `Columns`. It always lists the selected ledgers' own columns; a run does not change it (owner, 2026-10-06). With no ledger chosen: `Choose a ledger and its columns are listed here.` While its one day is fetched: six shimmer rows and no words, as the ledger list. If that day does not arrive: warn tint, `The columns of {ledger} did not arrive.`, with the button `Fetch the columns again`. A chosen ledger with no days on this site: `{ledger} has no days on this site yet, so it has no columns to list.` Names in `--font-data`; from row 11 each ledger's columns stand under one heading naming the ledger, and each type is in its family's colour, which replaced the tertiary colour on the owner's ruling of 2026-10-05. When a ledger is chosen, its family's description prints once, above its columns |
| 8 | Recent runs | A closed `<details>` directly under the action line at every width, summarised `Asked in this browser`, with the `history` icon (row 5). One line per run, newest first: `{dayMonth} {HH:MM} UTC - {rows} rows in {t} - {ledgers}`. With none: `Nothing asked in this browser yet.` Pressing an entry puts it in the editor exactly as a chip does, and runs nothing |
| 9 | The ledger list | Heading `Ledgers`, with `Refresh` and the `list-refresh` icon at its right end. Under the heading, one line: `Each ledger is one table the pipeline writes as it runs.` A Refresh's report replaces that line until the next refresh: `Refreshed. {ledger} now reaches {date}, {rows} more rows.` or `Refreshed. Nothing has moved since this page opened.` Then the filter: label `Filter ledgers`, placeholder `Name or family`, the `search` icon, `--text-base`; with no match, `No ledger matches "{text}".` Then `{k} of {n} ledgers chosen`, where `n` counts the ledgers that can be chosen. Then the published ledgers, in name order, each on one line where it fits - the name in `--text-sm`, the rest in `--text-xs`; a line breaks only between the name and `through {date}`, never inside either, and the wrapped part stands under the name, not under the checkbox, so in the 224 px side column most rows take two lines: a checkbox; the name exactly as a statement quotes it, in `--font-data`; in the tertiary colour, `through {shortDate(through)}`, from `SpanCost.through`; and the family's name only where it differs from the ledger's, as in `archive - content-similarity-judge`, because 16 of the 18 families hold one ledger named as the family. Then a closed `<details>`, `Not on this site ({n})`, holding the rest in the same one-line form with no checkbox. Loading: six shimmer rows, no words. The registry absent: `The list of ledgers is not on this site, so there is nothing to pick yet.` The registry failing to arrive: warn tint, `The list of ledgers did not arrive.`, with the button `Fetch the list again`. Jony's one-line row and disclosure replace Susan's two-line row and disabled checkbox; the words are hers. From row 11 a chosen ledger's row is tinted `--tint-accent` with a 3 px `--color-accent` edge at its start (owner, 2026-10-05) |
| 10 | Save, and what storage allows | `Save`, with the `saved` icon, turns the action line into a field labelled `Name`, filled with the statement's first line, at most `console.explorer_save_name_max_chars` (40) characters, with `Keep` and `Cancel`; there is no dialog (row 5). When the browser blocks storage: `This browser is not letting the page keep anything, so Save and the history are off.` `Copy link` stands at the right end of this panel's heading line, as the answer panel's copy buttons stand on theirs, and below 1024 px under the note (row 5) |
| 11 | Before anything has run | With no address, the span is the one this browser last used on the console. The editor holds this browser's most recent run with its ledgers chosen (row 5); with no recent run, it holds the first example whose ledgers are all published. Nothing runs. The page fetches only one day of each chosen ledger, to list its columns. The answer and chart panels hold the no-answer boxes in section 2.12, which carry `data-explorer-idle`. The captures picture this state at rest, and the "ready" pictures are taken after pressing Run on that default question. At 390 px the question panel starts at or below the bottom of an 844 px screen - an estimate from the measured 390 px rows less the 81 px price row that row 1 removes - so nothing that can grow long stands above the editor |
| 12 | The span sentence on this route | On this page the shared sentence under the band describes the next run. `WindowSource` in `frontend/src/lib/console/window-slot.ts` gains an optional `statusLine`, which `WindowStatus.svelte` prints instead of its own sentence, and this route sets `Run reads {d} UTC days, {dayMonth(from)} to {shortDate(to)}.` Its `monthsFor` returns 0, so no span tile is priced here; the action line prices the run in bytes. Otherwise the shared sentence would say `Every windowed section below is showing 14 days.` on a page that shows nothing until Run, and again after the reader changes the span without running. Susan's words; Jony asked for the same mechanism |

## 3. Rows

### Row #1 - The span control shrinks and the strip gains a sixth tab

- **Scope:** `WindowControl.svelte` becomes five short tiles reading `1D 7D 14D 30D 90D` with no standing label and no per-tile price; `console.default_window_days` becomes 14; `RouteId` gains `data-explorer` on both sides; the strip draws a sixth tab behind `console.data_explorer_tab`, default off. **Every console route takes the control changes**, which is the cost and is stated in the pull request. `WindowControlSource.svelte` is unchanged: it draws nothing and only passes the page's span to the layout.

| # | Now | Becomes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `Days shown` printed beside the tiles at every width | Gone from the eye. The `<legend class="sr-only">` stays, so a screen reader still hears the group's name, and the `D` on each tile carries the unit |
| 2 | Tiles read `1 7 14 30 90`, and a screen reader hears the number and a hidden " days" | `1D 7D 14D 30D 90D`. Each tile is `<span aria-hidden="true">{p}D</span><span class="sr-only">{p} days</span>`, with `day` for 1, so a screen reader says "14 days" and never "14D days" (Susan, 2026-10-02) |
| 3 | Every tile reserves a second line for a `+2 months` price whether or not one is due | Gone from the tiles. **The price moves into the span sentence under the band**, `WindowStatus.svelte`. With no wider preset priced: `Every windowed section below is showing {N} days.` With some, it adds ` {P}D would fetch {n} more month` (or `months`) for the narrowest priced preset, then `, {P}D {n} more` for each wider one, then a full stop - for example `Every windowed section below is showing 14 days. 30D would fetch 1 more month, 90D 3 more.` The old second sentence, `A month count on a preset is what picking it will fetch.`, is deleted, because no tile shows a count any more. The busy sentence stays as it is. Susan's words, 2026-10-02; Jony asked for the same move with `30 days` in place of `30D`, and the words are Susan's call |
| 4 | The control is as large as its label and its price room make it | **Five tiles at the existing 2.75rem (44 px) minimum both ways, 4 px apart: 236 x 44 px at every width.** Each tile's side padding drops from `--space-2` to `--space-1`, so its content box is 34 px wide: a three-character label measures 25.6 px in Segoe UI at weight 600 (a reading, 2026-10-02), and with 8 px of side padding a font with slightly wider digits would push a tile past 44 px. Below 1024 px the control keeps its own row under the tabs, at the start of the row; from 1024 px it keeps the right end of the one-row strip. At 390 px every console page's strip is then 267 px tall, the height measured without prices, so the Pipelines strip shrinks by 81 px (Jony, 2026-10-02) |
| 5 | `default_window_days` is 30, in `config/appearance.json` and in the legacy `console` block of `config/idhazh.json` | 14 in both, and `page_weight.cold_console_load_bytes` comes down from 3,400,000 to 2,300,000 with it (section 2.13) |
| 6 | `RouteId` names five routes | Six. One `ChangelogEntry` at the top, the oldest entry becomes the git-history pointer, `version` moves with the newest entry, no migration (section 2.2) |
| 7 | Five tabs | **What a sixth tab does to the strip** (Jony, 2026-10-02). The room for tabs is the screen width less the page margin on both sides; each tab starts at 128 px, with 8 px gaps. Below 435 px tabs stand two to a row: five stood 2-2-1, with Voices alone and stretched, and six stand 2-2-2, so `Records` fills the empty half of the third row - and the strip still grows, 267 to 323 px at 390 px, because Voices then shares its row and its worst state wraps in the 175 px tab (measured 2026-10-02 with the flag on; the estimate here had said it does not grow). From 435 to 730 px, six tabs stand in two rows, as five did. **From 731 to 871 px five tabs fit on one row and six do not**: `Records` stands alone on a second row, stretched across the full 707 px at 768 px, so every console page is one row of tabs, about 52 px, taller at those widths. From 872 px six fit on one row, and from 1024 px the strip stays the single scrolling row it is today. From 1024 px a tab is as wide as its label or its worst-state words: on 2026-10-02 the six needed 839 px and the row had 708 px at 1024 px, so all six show only from 1,156 px (a reading in Chromium on Windows; the words change daily). `Data explorer` is last, so on load `ConsoleNav.svelte` brings the active tab into view first, and the worst route only where the active tab stays whole; at those widths the worst route can open cut off at the left, and the band under the strip still names it (Jony, 2026-10-02). Susan proposed forcing two rows of three from 640 to 1023 px; the arrangement is Jony's call, and his keeps one row from 872 px |

- **Files touched:**
  - `frontend/src/lib/components/WindowControl.svelte` (the label, the tile text and its screen-reader text, the price room and its `priceRoom` prop and styles)
  - `frontend/src/lib/components/ChoiceTiles.svelte` (new: a radio group of 2.75rem tiles, each item `{value, shown, spoken}`, with the tile, selected, disabled and focus rules moved out of `WindowControl.svelte`, which then draws its five tiles through it; row 5's type switch is its second user, and row 5 adds the icon each of its tiles draws, because `icons.spec.ts` finds only a literal icon id and a generic tile cannot write one - corrected 2026-10-02 from `{value, shown, spoken, icon?}`, an optional field no row-1 tile reads)
  - `frontend/src/lib/components/WindowStatus.svelte` (the price sentence in the words of table row 3, and the old month-count sentence goes. It keeps room for three lines of `--leading-xs` below `frame.breakpoints_px[0]` (640 px) and two from 640 px up: the longest case - 1D chosen and all four wider presets priced, 111 characters - took three lines at 320 px and two from 360 px, measured 2026-10-02 in Chromium on Windows, which corrected the estimate of three at 390 px)
  - `frontend/src/lib/components/ConsoleNav.svelte` (the sixth tab, drawn only when the flag is on; on load the strip brings the active tab into view first, and the worst route only where the active tab stays whole)
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/+layout.svelte` (it stops passing `priceRoom`, hands `WindowStatus` the price, and draws the strip from `stripRoutes()`, which drops the `data-explorer` route while `console.data_explorer_tab` is off)
  - `frontend/src/routes/console/+page.server.ts` (its `windowPriced: true` goes with the prop it fed)

  - `frontend/src/lib/console/band.ts` (`RouteId`, `ROUTE_IDS`, `ROUTE_WORDS`, the module docstring which says "The console is five routes", and `stripRoutes(routes, dataExplorerTab)`, the pure filter the layout calls)
  - `config/appearance.json`, `backend/idhazh/contracts/knobs/console.py` (`default_window_days` 30 -> 14 with its description saying why; `data_explorer_tab` with its removal condition on the declaring line)

  - `frontend/src/lib/server/config.ts` (the `ConsoleConfig` type and the fallbacks for `default_window_days` and `data_explorer_tab`)

  - `frontend/tests/console-window.spec.ts`, `console-window-claims.spec.ts`, `console-chrome.spec.ts`, `console-shell.spec.ts`, `console-band.spec.ts`, and `console-nav.spec.ts` (`STRIP_WIDTHS` gains `{ width: 768, height: 1000, rows: 1 }`, the five-tab strip at a width no strip test checks today, and row 4 turns it to two rows; and `stripRoutes()` is checked without a page: five routes with the flag off, six with it on)

Found by `git grep -n -e default_window_days -e 3,400,000 -e '3.4 MB' -- docs` on 2026-10-02; the row re-runs that search
- **Acceptance gates:** local `npm --prefix frontend run test:changed -- --list` then the selected checks; `ruff check .`, `mypy backend`, `pytest backend/tests/contracts backend/tests/test_appearance_config.py backend/tests/test_console_payloads_producer.py -q`; the browser smoke on all five existing console routes at 390, 768 and 1440 in both themes, with the flag off on the built site and with it on in a local build. CI runs the full suite, including the bundle gate at the new ceiling.
- **Oracle:** **the control got smaller, nothing below it moved, and an old payload still draws a console.** Measured in a browser on the built console at 390, 768 and 1440 in both themes: the control measures 236 x 44 px at every width, below 1024 px at the start of its own row and from 1024 px at the right end of the strip; at 390 px the strip is 267 px tall on every console page; the chrome above the first panel is unchanged or shorter at 768 and 1440 px on every route and at 390 px on Pipelines, and at most 16 px taller at 390 px on the four routes that never price a span - the third line row 1's `WindowStatus.svelte` keeps below 640 px for its longest form (oracle corrected 2026-10-02 from "the page's total height is unchanged or smaller", which that decision cannot meet; the content below the chrome is not compared, because it follows the data the build reads) - and with the flag on, in the local build, exactly one more row of tabs is allowed at 731 to 871 px and nowhere else; and, starting on 1D so that every wider preset is priced, **selecting each of the five presets in turn moves no element below the strip by more than one pixel** - the property the reserved price room was bought for and the one a smaller control could silently lose. At 1024 px every console page opens with its own tab whole. The accessible name of the 14-day tile is `14 days`. Separately, the band fixture `tests/fixtures/contracts/console-band/newest-day.json`, which names five routes, validates against the new contract unchanged, and `stripRoutes()` returns five routes with the flag off and six with it on - the sixth from `ROUTE_WORDS`, with no worst state - checked without a page in `console-nav.spec.ts`, because the flag reaches the strip through each route's prerendered page data and a second build is not a test. It cannot settle whether the new shape reads better, nor whether `Data explorer` is the right word; Susan and Reader do.
- **Decisions:**

  | # | Decision | Authority |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | 1 | **The reference's shape is the shape**: five short tiles, no label, no box, no icon | Owner, 2026-09-28 |
  | 2 | **The unit lives on the tile, not in a label.** `1D` says the same thing in one character that `Days shown` says in ten, and the screen-reader legend is untouched. The `D` is hidden from screen readers and hidden text says `days`, so the tile is heard as "14 days" | Susan; the markup, Susan, 2026-10-02 |
  | 3 | **The price keeps a permanent home, and it is the span sentence under the band.** A price that appears and disappears moved seven panels by 15 px when it landed or cleared, measured 2026-09-09. Moving it to a line that is always drawn, with room kept for its longest form, keeps that fixed and takes the height back. **What it costs**: on a phone the price stands a band's height below the control, about 300 px - an estimate from the measured 390 px rows - and from 1024 px, while the strip is stuck to the top of the screen, the price is off screen, so a reader far down Pipelines sees the price only after choosing | Susan, with Jony; the measurement is the control's own; the cost stated by Jony, 2026-10-02 |
  | 4 | **14 days is the default on every route.** One default, or two routes answer the same question over different spans and a reader cannot compare them. **What it costs, named by the field's own description on `main`**: 30 was chosen over 14 "because the page states its own retirement rules over fourteen days, so a window equal to the rule shows the rule with no margin either side of it". At 14 a route that states a 14-day rule opens with no margin around it, and a reader widens to 30 with one press. The description is rewritten to give the new reason and keep that cost | Owner, 2026-09-28; the cost restated in the 2026-10-02 review |
  | 5 | **The control change and the tab ship in one pull request.** They are the two edits the console chrome takes, they share `config/appearance.json` and `knobs/console.py`, and splitting them buys a second review of the same file | Fowler |
  | 6 | **The label is `Data explorer`.** The owner ruled on 2026-10-04 that every visible string, page title, panel title, aria label, doc, test name and the backend-published tab label use Data explorer. The route id stays `data-explorer`. | Owner, 2026-10-04 |
  | 7 | **The tab ships behind a flag in `appearance.json` until the page exists.** A tab pointing at a route that 404s is worse than no tab | Guardrail #6; Fowler |
  | 8 | **This row draws no panel and registers none.** `panel-captures.spec.ts` pictures every panel `console.panel_groups` names, so the group is registered by row 4, which draws the panels; the contract refuses a `judged_panel_ids` entry no route draws, which is why row 6 adds them there | Fowler, 2026-10-02 review |
  | 9 | **The legacy `console.default_window_days` in `config/idhazh.json` moves with the appearance file, and the cold-load ceiling comes down with it.** The two copies must agree, and the ceiling's own test refuses one that has room for a whole extra shard. Deleting the legacy key instead would leave `bundle-gate.mjs` with no window to price | Fowler, 2026-10-02 review |

- **Rejected alternatives:**

  | # | Option | Why rejected | What it would cost to take | Authority |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 1 | Fork a smaller control for this page only | Two controls for one question, and two windows a reader cannot compare | Zero to take; costs the single-window property | Susan |
  | 2 | Keep the label and only drop the price room | Saves the vertical and not the horizontal, and the horizontal is what pushes the control off the strip when a sixth tab arrives | Zero; costs the strip its room | Susan |
  | 3 | Drop the price entirely | The wide span costs month files and a reader would meet the cost after choosing it | Zero; costs the reader the price before they pay it | Carmack |
  | 4 | Ship the control and the tab as two pull requests | They edit the same two config surfaces, so the second would take the first in anyway. Two reviews of one file | Zero; costs a review round | Fowler |
  | 5 | No tab; reach the page by link only | An operator surface nobody can find is an operator surface nobody uses | Zero; costs the page its readers | Jony |
  | 6 | Label it `Explore` | A verb among five nouns. The strip stops being a list of subjects | Zero; costs the strip its grammar | Susan |
  | 7 | Delete the legacy `console` block from `config/idhazh.json` while here | It is the read-side migration's middle layer for an unmigrated config, `test_the_legacy_blocks_still_validate_so_an_unmigrated_config_still_reads` holds it, and the bundle gate and `test_page_ceilings.py` read the window from it | A change to two gates and a migration rule, for no capability this plan needs | Fowler, 2026-10-02 review |

### Row #2 - The door answers a written question, on the engine plan 51 shipped

- **Scope:** `ask()` and `askCost()` join `slice()` and `ledgerReach()` in `frontend/src/lib/data/ledger.ts`; `LEDGER_NAMES` widens to every ledger `config/ledgers.json` declares; `statement.ts` refuses anything that is not one read-only statement; `slice.ts` gains a second pure function for the writers' tier, which only `ask()` calls (section 2.7). `RawDayIndex` gains its optional `bytes` list, and the door reads a listing only up to the newest day the build listed, from `__RAW_LISTED_THROUGH__`. Until row 7 stages a listing that constant names no day, so `ask()` reads packed files only; no page calls `ask()` before row 4, which waits for row 7. **`engine.ts` is not touched** - no setting, no pre-load, no pin (section 2.6). **No page, no chart, no publishing, and no config file** - this is the reader, shipped with its own witness.

- **Files touched:**
  - `frontend/src/lib/data/ledger.ts` (`ask`, `askCost`; it binds the same keeper and engine `slice()` uses, and `__RAW_LISTED_THROUGH__`)
  - `frontend/src/lib/data/slice-shapes.ts` (`LEDGER_NAMES` widens to every registry ledger; `AskOptions`, `AskResult`, `AskFault`, `AskRefusal`, `Column`, `FetchCost`, `SpanCost` with its `through`)
  - `frontend/src/lib/data/ask-reader.ts` (new: hold the files, create and drop the views, run `DESCRIBE` and then the statement inside the wraps of section 2.5 rules 7 and 8, one call at a time, call `done()`, and answer an engine that does not start with `unreachable` and `fault` `engine` - the body `ledger.ts` calls, beside `slice-reader.ts`)
  - `frontend/src/lib/data/statement.ts` (new: is this one read-only statement no longer than `maxChars`? It answers the `AskRefusal` that refuses it, or `null` - pure, no engine import, unit-testable on its own; its docstring says it is a courtesy and not the boundary)
  - `frontend/src/lib/data/slice.ts` (the writers'-tier function beside `filesFor()`, which is not changed; it takes the newest listed day per ledger as an argument)
  - `frontend/src/lib/data/slice-reader.ts` (the two raw-tier paths, composed only from a closed ledger name, a checked day and a name matching the file-name grammar)
  - `frontend/src/lib/data/slice-query.ts` (`listOf()` becomes an export, unchanged)
  - `frontend/src/lib/data/raw-day-index.ts` (new: the hand copy of `RawDayIndex` with `bytes` required, its stamp, and the file-name grammar it checks; a listing with no `version` reads as this stamp, as an index with none does in `compact-index.ts`)
  - `frontend/src/lib/console/waiting.ts` (`PanelState` gains `refused`, with its row in the table of states and its sentence)
  - `frontend/src/lib/charts/d3/empty.ts` (it re-exports each state's sentence, and gains `refusedSentence`)
  - `frontend/scripts/raw-listed-through.mjs` (new: how far do the listings the copy step staged reach, per ledger? - the newest `state/raw/<ledger>/index/<YYYY-MM-DD>.json` under `frontend/static/`, and no ledger when none is staged)
  - `frontend/vite.config.ts`, `frontend/src/app.d.ts` (`__RAW_LISTED_THROUGH__`, from that script)
  - `backend/idhazh/contracts/ledger_index.py` (`RawDayIndex.bytes`: optional, one size per name in `files`, in the same order, each zero or more, and refused by name when the counts differ; the class docstring and `listed_at`'s description name the second writer, the site build, which stages a listing for a day not packed yet into the site only; one changelog line - `bytes: an optional size per listed file, which the site build fills` - and the new `version`. The digest stays over the names alone, so every listing already written keeps its digest)
  - `tests/fixtures/contracts/raw-day-index/` (each of the three fixtures gains the field, because the round-trip test dumps every field and must reproduce the file byte for byte; `a-populated-day.json` carries a size per file)
  - `backend/tests/contracts/test_ledger_index.py` (a listing with one size per name validates; a count that differs, or a negative size, is refused by name; a listing without the field validates)
  - `frontend/tests/ledger-door.spec.ts` (widened), `frontend/tests/statement.spec.ts` (new), `frontend/tests/explorer-boundary.spec.ts` (new), `frontend/tests/raw-listed-through.spec.ts` (new)
  - `frontend/tests/support/explorer-page/index.html` and `explorer.ts` (new: the page the browser check builds, beside `door-page/`)
  - `frontend/tests/support/range-host.ts` (exports `addonCache()` so the boundary and range specs serve the DuckDB add-on locally)

  - `tests/fixtures/ledger-door/` (a second ledger with all three indexes and the files they name, so a statement can join two - corrected at review: `item-health` is a byte copy of `host-fingerprint`'s compact files under its own paths and indexes, not rebuilt through the compaction, because the door reads indexes and rows and never an envelope, and the README says so; an unpacked day after its newest packed day, with a `RawDayIndex` that carries `bytes` and two writer files, one holding a row whose cells carry the hostile text in section 2.10; a day both packed and listed, which must be read once; and `answers/<case>.json`, the rows each oracle case must return), with `tests/fixtures/ledger-door/README.md`
  - `backend/tests/contracts/test_ledger_door_fixture.py` (it holds one `host-fingerprint` root today; it gains the second ledger, checks each listing against the files it names and their sizes, and recomputes every `answers/<case>.json` with DuckDB in Python and holds it equal)
  - `backend/tests/contracts/test_frontend_index_shapes.py` (the hand copy in `raw-day-index.ts` against `RawDayIndex`, beside the copy in `compact-index.ts`; `test_every_ledger_the_door_may_query_is_a_ledger` holds `LEDGER_NAMES` equal to `LedgerName`, section 2.4)
  - `backend/tests/contracts/test_published_ledgers_cover_the_panels.py` (`test_every_ledger_the_door_can_be_asked_for_is_published` is deleted and the module docstring's last sentence goes with it, section 2.4; the panel test stays)
  - `frontend/tests/chart-vocabulary.spec.ts` (the single-engine walk still finds one importer)
  - `docs/architecture/publishing/how-the-query-door-answers-a-written-question.md` (new: the second entry point, the views, the wraps, the writers' tier, and what holds the boundary - a page of its own, because the existing page answers how the door answers a panel, and row 3 edits that page at the same time)
  - `docs/agents/bootstrap.md` (one routing row for the new page)
  - `docs/architecture/contracts/schemas.md` (`RawDayIndex` gains `bytes`; its hand copy is `raw-day-index.ts`)
  - `docs/concepts/console-design/the-mark-shapes-a-panel-may-reach-for.md` (in the paragraph headed "A floor missed is "too few", never "refused"", the clause saying that "Refused" and `unreachable` already mean a fetch that did not come back becomes: "Unreachable means a fetch that did not come back, and refused means a question the page or the engine would not run; a floor missed is neither.")
  - `docs/how-to/move-a-ledger-to-parquet.md` (its console-readers line: every ledger `config/ledgers.json` declares is already in `LEDGER_NAMES`; a page may read one once `ledger.published` names it) and `docs/architecture/contracts/ledger-registry.md` (a new `LedgerName` member also joins `LEDGER_NAMES` in `frontend/src/lib/data/slice-shapes.ts`, which `test_every_ledger_the_door_may_query_is_a_ledger` holds equal to `LedgerName`)
  - `docs/reference/agent-notes/browser.md` (records why worker content-policy log entries need a CDP session and why blocked requests still surface as `requestfailed`)
  - `pyproject.toml` (corrected at dispatch: the fixture answers are recomputed with DuckDB in Python, so `duckdb>=1.5.5` belongs in the `dev` extra instead of relying on the shared local venv)
- **Acceptance gates:** local `npm --prefix frontend run test:changed -- --list` then the selected checks; `pytest backend/tests/contracts -q`, `ruff check .`, `mypy backend`. **One browser check, because it is the only place the boundary can be proven**: `frontend/tests/explorer-boundary.spec.ts` builds `frontend/tests/support/explorer-page/` (`index.html`, `explorer.ts`) with Vite and serves it from 127.0.0.1, as `ledger-ranges.spec.ts` serves its page. SvelteKit writes its content policy only into its own pages, and the support page is a plain Vite page - `door-page/index.html` carries no policy at all - so the spec writes into `index.html` the meta tag SvelteKit would write, from the `kit.csp.directives` it imports out of `frontend/svelte.config.js` (its `connect-src` from `connectSources()` in `asset-base.js`), and the page runs under the shipped policy. It runs `ask()` with `SELECT * FROM read_csv('https://example.invalid/x.csv')` and asserts that the answer is `refused`; a second DevTools session attached to the worker receives a log line naming `connect-src` and that address, because Playwright's page console stream cannot see worker log entries; every request Playwright sees for that address fails with `csp`, at least one such request exists, and none gets a response. CI runs the full suite. No panel smoke - this row draws nothing.
- **Oracle:** **a written question over two ledgers returns exactly the right rows, and nothing the call did not select is reachable by name.** In one Node run on the fixture ledgers: a statement joining two ledgers returns exactly the rows in `tests/fixtures/ledger-door/answers/<case>.json`, which `test_ledger_door_fixture.py` recomputes with DuckDB in Python and holds equal; a date present in both a packed day and a writer file is read once; the unpacked day returns its rows and its date appears in `unpackedDays`; a day after the newest listed day is clamped away, and a missing listing inside the listed range answers `unreachable` at its day; a zero-row span returns `quiet` with its columns; a date named by no tier returns `unreachable`; an answer past `maxRows` returns `capped` with exactly `maxRows` rows, and an `ORDER BY` statement keeps its order under the cap; a statement that ends in a `--` comment runs, and so does `SELECT 1; -- done`; `sum()`, `1.5`, a date, a list and a struct each answer as the engine's text; `SUMMARIZE` answers, and `EXPLAIN` answers its two text columns; a join with two `id` columns keeps both; a selected ledger with no file in the span answers through its empty view; a span whose indexes and listings exceed `maxFetchBytes` returns `refused` **with no data file fetched**; a statement longer than `maxChars` returns `refused` with no engine started; the last three statements in section 2.6 are refused for the reason named beside each; **a second call selecting one ledger cannot read the ledger the first call selected through its view name**, which is the view-drop rule; and two calls made at once run one after the other. It cannot settle whether the content policy refuses a request made from inside the engine's worker - no Node run can, which is why that one assertion is the browser check in the gates above. Nor does it settle that a minted file name is unreadable, which section 2.6 says no control claims.
- **Decisions:**

  | # | Decision | Authority |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | 1 | **No seal. The boundary is the browser's content policy** - plan 51's approved design, and the one every static site uses. A database setting would be a second lock on a door the browser already holds shut, and closing the network would break the engine's own add-on fetch | Owner, 2026-09-29; section 2.6 |
  | 2 | **`engine.ts` is not edited by this row.** It is shared with every panel, and a row that needs no change there makes none | Fowler |
  | 3 | **`statement.ts` is named a courtesy in the plan and in its own docstring.** A refusal list nothing rests on has to say so, or the next reader takes it for the boundary and starts widening it | Andre, with Carmack |
  | 4 | **A registered buffer is a file, so the door creates one view per selected ledger and drops every unselected one on every call.** Without the drop, a view from an earlier run lets a statement read a ledger the operator did not select and did not see the cost of | Fowler; section 2.4 |
  | 5 | **The operator's text is a statement, and the structured predicate stays for panels.** The door refused raw SQL because the caller there is a panel and fetched text could reach it. The caller here is a person typing into their own browser, and the text they type never leaves it. Both are true at once, which is why this is a second entry point and not a change to the first | Fowler and Andre, 2026-09-28; owner asked for the box |
  | 6 | **The minted file name and the ledger name are two layers, not two options.** One ledger is many files, so a registered name could never have been a ledger's name; the view is what binds the set to its files | Fowler; section 2.4 |
  | 7 | **The engine takes a caret range, not a pin.** No boundary here is a setting, so no release can remove one | Carmack |
  | 8 | **This row reads no config file, and its three ceilings arrive as arguments.** The knobs that bound the page are minted by the row that reads them, row 4, so this row's tests pass literals | Fowler |
  | 9 | **`ask()` goes through the page keeper and never calls `engine.register()` or `engine.registerAddress()`.** The keeper is what stops a file crossing the network or entering the engine twice, and in a browser registering takes the buffer, so a second registration would read an empty file | Carmack; section 2.1 module 5 |
  | 10 | **`LEDGER_NAMES` widens to the whole registry, and the two existing tests that read it are changed to say so** (section 2.4). A ledger declared and not published answers `missing` by name, which is more useful than being absent from the list | Fowler; the deletion of the closed-set half of `test_published_ledgers_cover_the_panels.py` follows from this decision, 2026-10-02 review |
  | 11 | **The writers' files are read through a listing in `RawDayIndex`'s shape, which gains an optional `bytes` list, one size per name in `files`.** This widens one persisted shape and declares none: an older listing still validates, so no migration is needed, and the change carries the new `version`, its changelog line, the three fixtures and the hand copy (CLAUDE.md section 11). The size is what lets `askCost()` price a writer file before any fetch, lets `ask()` refuse an oversized span before any fetch, and keeps the page keeper's length check on every file it holds. The two other answers cost more: names only would make the keeper accept a file of unknown size - in a module every panel shares - so a cut-short writer file would no longer be caught and the price of those days would be a guess; packed files only would stop the page at the newest packed day, 2026-09-28 for the eval ledger until plan 57's first row lands | Fowler, 2026-10-02, adopted to make the plan executable; the owner may overrule |
  | 12 | **`filesFor()` is not changed; the writers' tier is a second function beside it.** `slice()` calls `filesFor()`, and panels stay on packed files (section 2.7) | Fowler, 2026-10-02 review |
  | 13 | **`ask()` puts the statement on lines of its own inside its wraps, casts every cell to text, and takes the columns from `DESCRIBE`** (section 2.5 rules 7 and 8). A one-line wrap breaks on a trailing `--` comment, `cellOf()` throws on most answers a person writes, and a quiet answer has no row to read column names from | Fowler, 2026-10-02, from probes on DuckDB 1.5.5 and DuckDB-Wasm 1.33.1-dev57 |
  | 14 | **The door learns how far the listings reach from the staged tree, through a script of its own** (section 2.7, listing rule 5). `raw-listed-through.mjs` reads what the copy step staged, so the constant cannot name a day the site lacks, and this row owns it without touching the copy step row 3 edits in the same wave | Fowler, 2026-10-02 |

- **Rejected alternatives:**

  | # | Option | Why rejected | What it would cost to take | Authority |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 1 | Seal the engine with `enable_external_access = false` and the two auto-load settings | It breaks the engine. The parquet reader is fetched over the network at first use, so the setting that closes the network closes every panel on the console. Pre-loading the add-on first would work, and would be two more moving parts holding up a property `connect-src` already holds on its own | Zero to write; costs every query, or costs two mechanisms for nothing | Owner, 2026-09-29 |
  | 2 | Pin the engine's version so a release cannot change a setting we rely on | We rely on no setting. A pin buys a slower upgrade path and nothing else | Zero; costs the normal upgrade path | Carmack |
  | 3 | Build the page's own engine module, separate from the door | Two importers of the engine, and the single-engine rule becomes unenforceable | Zero; costs the single-engine rule | Fowler |
  | 4 | Register each ledger's files under a name the caller chooses, so no view is needed | The engine mints names precisely so no caller and no fetched text can name a file - and it would not work anyway, because one ledger is many files and they cannot share a name | Zero; costs the minting rule and does not remove the view | Carmack |
  | 5 | Keep the structured predicate and add operators until it can express a join | It becomes a query language of our own, badly, and an operator has to learn it | Months, and the result is worse than SQL | Owner, 2026-09-28 |
  | 6 | Parse the statement ourselves and allow only what we understand | A SQL parser is a dependency or a mistake. The boundary is the content policy, which the browser enforces on the page and on the engine's worker (section 2.6); a parser would be a dependency holding up a courtesy | A parser dependency, and a false sense of a boundary | Carmack |
  | 7 | Read only packed files, as panels do | The answer stops two days short of now, which is the question an operator most often opens this page to ask | Zero; costs the page its point | Owner, 2026-09-28 |
  | 8 | **Let a statement name an `https://` address directly**, by loading the remote-file add-on and widening `connect-src` to the open web | The content policy is the only boundary this page has, so widening it to the open web removes it. **What the reader would lose by not having it**: writing a URL inside the statement rather than picking dates and letting the door choose the address - a keystroke, not a capability | Zero to write; costs the page its only boundary | Carmack and Andre; ESCALATE trigger 2 |
